    -- WHY THIS EXISTS
    --   Ten plugins each carried their own `io.open` + `vim.json.decode` + pcall
    --   dance to persist state (tasks, launch profiles, breakpoints, favorites,
    --   workspaces, fonts, nuget cache...). Same six lines, ten chances to forget a
    --   `pcall` and hard-error the editor on a truncated file.
    --
    -- CONTRACT
    --   * Reads never throw. A missing or corrupt file yields the caller's fallback.
    --   * Writes create parent directories and return ok/err instead of throwing.
    --   * `nil` is never written: `save` refuses non-table values.
    --
    -- USAGE
    --   local store = require("fox.core.store")
    --   local data = store.load(path, {})           -- always a table here
    --   local ok, err = store.save(path, data)      -- creates parent dirs
    -- ============================================================================
    
    local path_util = require("fox.core.path")
    
    local M = {}
    
    -- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    -- Reading
    -- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    
    --- Reads a whole file as a string.
    ---
    --- @param filepath string Path to read.
    --- @return string|nil content File contents, or nil when unreadable.
    function M.read_file(filepath)
        local f = io.open(filepath, "r")
        if not f then
            return nil
        end
        local content = f:read("*a")
        f:close()
        return content
    end
    
    --- Loads and decodes a JSON file.
    --- Never throws: unreadable, empty and malformed files all return `fallback`.
    ---
    --- @param filepath string Path to a JSON file.
    --- @param fallback any Value returned when the file cannot be decoded. Default `{}`.
    --- @return any data Decoded table, or `fallback`.
    function M.load(filepath, fallback)
        if fallback == nil then
            fallback = {}
        end
        local content = M.read_file(filepath)
        if not content or content == "" then
            return fallback
        end
        local ok, data = pcall(vim.json.decode, content)
        if ok and type(data) == "table" then
            return data
        end
        return fallback
    end
    
    -- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    -- Deterministic encoding
    -- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    -- `vim.json.encode` walks object tables with `pairs`, whose order depends on
    -- Lua's hash implementation -- the same table can serialize with its keys in
    -- a different order on every run, which shows up as spurious git diffs on
    -- config files that never actually changed. Object keys are sorted here;
    -- arrays keep their natural order since it is meaningful.
    
    --- Encodes `value` as JSON with object keys sorted for stable output.
    --- @param value any
    --- @return string
    local function encode_sorted(value)
        if type(value) ~= "table" then
            return vim.json.encode(value)
        end
        if next(value) == nil or vim.islist(value) then
            local parts = {}
            for i, item in ipairs(value) do
                parts[i] = encode_sorted(item)
            end
            return "[" .. table.concat(parts, ",") .. "]"
        end
    
        local keys = {}
        for key in pairs(value) do
            table.insert(keys, key)
        end
        table.sort(keys, function(a, b)
            return tostring(a) < tostring(b)
        end)
    
        local parts = {}
        for _, key in ipairs(keys) do
            table.insert(parts, vim.json.encode(tostring(key)) .. ":" .. encode_sorted(value[key]))
        end
        return "{" .. table.concat(parts, ",") .. "}"

    ---
    name: ponytail
    description: >
      Lazy senior dev mode: the smallest change that fully solves the task, and a
      reply a busy human understands in one read. Use on any coding task (writing,
      fixing, refactoring, reviewing, choosing dependencies) and when the user says
      "ponytail", "be lazy", "simplest solution", "yagni", or complains about
      over-engineering or bloat. Levels: lite, full (default), ultra.
    argument-hint: "[lite|full|ultra|off]"
    license: MIT
    ---
    
    # Ponytail
    
    You are a lazy senior developer. The best code is the code never written. You solve the whole problem with the least new code. End your reply with one or two lines: what you skipped or did not check, and any risk the user must know.
    
    Active for the whole session until the user says "stop ponytail" or "normal mode". Switch level: `/ponytail lite|full|ultra`.
    
    ## Before you write
    
    Read the task and the code it touches. List every place your change must reach: callers, tests, fixtures, config, exports. Check what your change could break for users: data it would destroy or expose, callers that stop working. That is scope. Extra features are not.
    
    ## The smallest complete change
    
    Take the first option that fully works:
    
    1. Does it need to exist? Skip features, options and flexibility nobody asked for, and name them in one line. A vague request ("build me X") gets the smallest version that does the core job.
    2. Already in this codebase (a helper, component, service, pattern)? Use it the way the surrounding code does.
    3. Standard library or a platform feature? Use it, unless the project has its own. A house component beats a native widget.
    4. An installed dependency? Use it. Never add a dependency for a few lines.
    5. Can it be one line a reader gets at a glance? One line.
    6. Otherwise: the minimum code that works.
    
    - Be lazy about the solution, never about the change itself: finish every part the task needs, including the callers, tests and fixtures your change breaks.
    - No abstraction, wrapper, type conversion, option, config, boilerplate or "for later" code nobody asked for. Keep values in the form the platform already gives you. Deletion beats addition. Keep the structure the codebase already has: its layers, interfaces and conventions.
    - Minimize the complete diff across all affected source files, preserving existing code, style, names and structure wherever they already work. Do not rewrite, reformat, modernize or switch patterns for a supposedly better approach unless explicitly requested or necessary to meet the task, correctness or safety. Do not count generated files when assessing diff size. A one-liner that needs decoding is not short.
    - Comment only the why the code cannot show, in one line.
    - Bug fix: before you edit, grep every caller of the function you touch, then fix the root cause once in the shared code.
    - Code you move or merge keeps its error handling and validation.
    - Between options of equal size, take the one that is correct on edge cases.
    - Lazy code without its check is unfinished: new non-trivial logic (a branch, a loop, a parser, money or security, or a whole new script or app) leaves one small test or an assert-based self-check. Trivial changes need none.
    - A shortcut with a known limit gets a code comment in this form: `shortcut: <the limit>, <when to upgrade>`.
    
    Never cut: validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs, anything the user asked for.
    
    ## Levels
    
    | Level | Behavior |
    |-------|----------|
    | **lite** | Build what was asked. Name the smaller option in one line and let the user pick. |
    | **full** | The rules above. Default. |
    | **ultra** | Also question the request: before building, push back on any part the need does not justify. |
    
    
    Default: "full"
    
    ---
    name: kl-solid-coding
    description: "Apply SOLID and KL's selected Go proverbs on every coding task in any language: write, fix, refactor, review, choose dependencies. Always use alongside Ponytail."
    metadata:
      display-name: KL-SOLID-CODING
    ---
    
    # SOLID coding
    
    # ALWAYS
    
    - Every coding task: load [Ponytail](../ponytail/SKILL.md) + relevant language/domain skills.
    - Current need; Ponytail's smallest complete solution. Go concurrency/panic guidance requires equivalent language semantics.
    
    ## SOLID meanings
    
    - **S — Single Responsibility Principle:** one reason to change per module, tied to one actor/business concern; group code changing together, separate independent concerns.
    - **O — Open/Closed Principle:** open for extension, closed for modification; extend stable boundaries without changing established behavior. Extension points require demonstrated variation.
    - **L — Liskov Substitution Principle:** substitutes preserve contracts: accepted inputs, outputs, invariants, failure behavior; no stronger preconditions/weaker postconditions.
    - **I — Interface Segregation Principle:** clients depend only on needed operations; small, cohesive, consumer-focused contracts.
    - **D — Dependency Inversion Principle:** high-level policy + low-level details depend on abstractions; details implement policy-facing contracts. Supply concrete dependencies at composition boundaries.
    
    ## Go proverbs
    
    - Don't communicate by sharing memory, share memory by communicating.
    - Concurrency is not parallelism.
    - Channels orchestrate; mutexes serialize.
    - The bigger the interface, the weaker the abstraction.
    - A little copying is better than a little dependency.
    - Clear is better than clever.
    - Reflection is never clear.
    - Don't just check errors, handle them gracefully.
    - Design the architecture, name the components, document the details.
    - Don't panic.
    
    # NEVER
    
    - No speculative interfaces, inheritance, layers, or dependencies for SOLID compliance; preserve required contracts, validation, error handling.
    
    # Taste
    
    - Use names that state the domain purpose of methods, variables and collections; avoid unexplained names such as `rules` or `reglas` outside framework-required contracts. Preserve framework names and existing public contracts.
    - Prefer consistent domain-first prefixes that group related variables/settings like namespaces, following the language casing convention. Example: `panel_zoom_enabled`, `panel_zoom_gesture_type`, `panel_zoom_factor`, `panel_zoom_slider_min`; keep the distinct panel-opening setting `panel_gesture` separate. Avoid reversed or ambiguous names such as `panel_gesture_zoom`. When renaming persisted keys, migrate existing values and update all callers.
    - Keep helpers private/file-local and at the end of their class or file. Shared behavior belongs in a named domain collaborator, not cross-file `helper_*` calls or generic utility bags.
    - Avoid unexplained numeric IDs and abstract values in conditions. Prefer meaningful strings where the domain permits; otherwise use a named constant or a preceding comment explaining the case. Preserve required external IDs.
    - PHP: import classes at the top of the file instead of repeating fully qualified class names inside methods, unless qualification is needed to disambiguate.
    - Add PHPDoc for non-obvious contracts, data shapes, constraints and business reasons; do not restate the signature or narrate the implementation. Check history when assessing stale comments; retain explanations of current constraints.
    - Keep related validation and special cases understandable together: do not silently skip several fields and handle them elsewhere under an unexplained check for only one of them.
    - Prefer a little local duplication over a dependency on a trivial wrapper that adds no meaningful responsibility. Do not extract a module solely to wrap a few lines.
    - Keep behavior specific to one case in its owning module or screen. Extract it only for demonstrated reuse or a boundary that materially improves clarity or correctness; one usage alone does not justify another file or layer.
    - Place reusable components in the project's component area, grouped in subfolders by module, domain or category. Keep screens in the screen area and compose them from those components. Apply these rules independently of language or framework.
    ✓ • 16ms

    -- ============================================================================
    -- TERMINAL CREATION
    -- ============================================================================
    
    --- `:terminal` command for the current project, routed through WSL when the
    --- project lives on a WSL path.
    --- @return string cmd
    local function terminal_open_cmd()
        local ok, wsl = pcall(require, "plugins.fox.tools.wsl")
        local wsl_cmd = ok and wsl.shell_command_for_cwd(vim.fn.getcwd()) or nil
        if wsl_cmd then
            return "terminal " .. wsl_cmd
        end
        -- Kitty starts the user's interactive shell; use the same initialization
        -- inside Neovim while keeping Bash for POSIX tool/task commands.
        local shell = vim.env.SHELL
        if vim.fn.has("unix") == 1 and shell and vim.fn.executable(shell) == 1 then
            return "terminal " .. vim.fn.fnameescape(shell) .. " -il"
        end
        return "terminal"
    end
    
    --- Shows terminal `n` in `win`, spawning the shell when the slot has no buffer.
    --- @param t table Slot record.
    --- @param n integer Slot number.
    --- @param win integer Window to fill.
    local function fill_window(t, n, win)
        if is_valid_win(win) then
            vim.api.nvim_set_current_win(win)
        end
    
        if is_valid_buf(t.buf) then
            vim.api.nvim_win_set_buf(win, t.buf)
            return
        end
    
        local cwd = vim.fn.getcwd()
        local stat_fn = (vim.uv or vim.loop).fs_stat
        if stat_fn(cwd .. "/.foxnvim/secondary_repos.json") then
            local ok_sec, sec = pcall(require, "fox.git.secondary")
            if ok_sec and sec then
                sec.setup_environment(cwd)
            end
        end
    
        vim.cmd(terminal_open_cmd())
        t.buf = vim.api.nvim_get_current_buf()
        vim.bo[t.buf].buflisted = false
        vim.b[t.buf].fox_term_num = n
        vim.b[t.buf].fox_is_multi_term = true
        vim.b[t.buf].fox_env_slot = _G._fox_active_env_slot or 1
    end
    
    -- ============================================================================
    -- FLOATING WINDOW HELPERS
    -- ============================================================================
    
    --- Checks if a window is a floating window.
    --- @param win integer|nil
    --- @param slot integer Target slot 1..9.
    --- @param callback function|nil
    function M.close_environment(slot, callback)
        slot = slot or M.get_active_slot()
        local env = _G._fox_environments[slot]
        if not env then
            notify("Environment slot #" .. slot .. " is not active", vim.log.levels.WARN)
            if callback then
                callback()
            end
            return
        end
        local query = package.loaded["fox.query"]
        if query and not query.can_replace(env.cwd) then
            return
        end
    
        local confirm_msg = string.format(
            "Close Environment #%d ('%s')?\nThis stops its scoped LSPs, terminals and unloads its buffers.",
            slot,
            env.name
        )
        if vim.fn.confirm(confirm_msg, "&Yes\n&No", 2) ~= 1 then
            if callback then
                callback()
            end
            return
        end
    
        -- 1. Stop scoped LSPs
        if query and not query.close_root(env.cwd) then
            return
        end
        M.stop_environment_lsps(env)
    
        -- 2. Kill scoped terminals
        kill_environment_terminals(env)
    
        -- 3. Delete buffers belonging to this slot
        for _, buf in ipairs(vim.api.nvim_list_bufs()) do
            if vim.api.nvim_buf_is_valid(buf) then
                if vim.b[buf].fox_env_slot == slot then
                    pcall(vim.api.nvim_buf_delete, buf, { force = true })
                end
            end
        end
    
        -- 5. Remove slot
        _G._fox_environments[slot] = nil
        sync_internal_state()

    -- ============================================================================
    -- fox.core.project -- Project root detection and `.foxnvim/` config resolution.
    -- ============================================================================
    -- WHY THIS EXISTS
    --   Four modules (tasks, launch_profiles, dap_breakpoints, type_injector) each
    --   re-implemented "walk up until a project marker, then look for my JSON file in
    --   .foxnvim / .foxlocal / .nvimfox". The lookup order is a user-visible contract:
    --   getting it wrong silently reads the wrong project's settings.
    --
    -- THE CONTRACT
    --   * Root = nearest ancestor directory containing a marker from `M.root_markers`.
    --   * A per-project config file is searched in `M.config_dirs`, in order.
    --   * When nothing exists yet, the FIRST candidate is returned so writers create
    --     `.foxnvim/<name>` by default.
    --
    -- USAGE
    --   local project = require("fox.core.project")
    --   local root = project.root()                          -- cwd-based fallback
    --   local file = project.config_path("tasks.json", root)  -- <root>/.foxnvim/tasks.json
    -- ============================================================================
    
    local path = require("fox.core.path")
    
    local M = {}
    
    -- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    -- Configuration -- tweak these two lists to change project detection globally
    -- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    
    --- Files/directories that mark a project root, searched upward from the buffer.
    --- Order matters only for readability; `vim.fs.find` returns the nearest match.
    M.root_markers = {
        ".foxnvim",
        ".vscode",
        ".nvimfox",
        "Makefile",
        "package.json",
        "Cargo.toml",
        ".git",
        "go.mod",
        "pyproject.toml",
    }
    
    --- Directories that may hold per-project FOX config, in lookup order.
    --- The first entry is also the write target when no config exists yet.
    M.config_dirs = { ".foxnvim", ".vscode", ".foxlocal", ".nvimfox" }
    
    -- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    -- API
    -- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    
    --- Resolves the project root for a buffer by walking up to the nearest marker.
    --- Falls back to the current working directory when no marker is found.
    ---
    --- @param bufnr integer|nil Buffer handle. Defaults to the current buffer.
    --- @return string root Project root directory (not normalized, matches `vim.fs`).
    function M.root(bufnr)
        local current = bufnr and path.buffer_dir(bufnr) or vim.fn.expand("%:p:h")
        if current == "" then
            return path.normalize(vim.fn.getcwd())
        end
    
        current = path.normalize(current)
        local cwd = path.normalize(vim.fn.getcwd())
    
        -- If current is inside cwd, search for root markers up to cwd first
        local rel = path.relative_to(current, cwd)
        if rel then
            local match = vim.fs.find(M.root_markers, { upward = true, path = current, stop = cwd })
            if match and #match > 0 then
                local found_dir = path.normalize(vim.fs.dirname(match[1]))
                if found_dir ~= cwd then
                    -- Check if cwd itself has a project root marker (.git, .foxnvim, go.mod, package.json, etc.)
                    local cwd_match = vim.fs.find(M.root_markers, { upward = false, path = cwd, limit = 1 })
                    if cwd_match and #cwd_match > 0 then
                        -- If found_dir is a git repository/submodule of its own, respect found_dir.
                        -- Otherwise, the main project workspace root (cwd) wins over subfolder markers.
                        local is_git_repo = path.is_dir(path.join(found_dir, ".git")) or path.is_file(path.join(found_dir, ".git"))
                        if not is_git_repo then
                            return cwd
                        end
                    end
                end
                return found_dir
            end
            return cwd
        end
    
        -- If current is outside cwd, search upward freely
        local match = vim.fs.find(M.root_markers, { upward = true, path = current })
        if match and #match > 0 then
            return path.normalize(vim.fs.dirname(match[1]))
        end
        return cwd
    end
    
    --- Resolves a per-project config file, e.g. `tasks.json` or `launch.json`.
    --- Returns the first existing candidate; when none exist, returns the path under
    --- the first entry of `M.config_dirs` so callers can create it.
    ---

        cat > lua/fox/terminal/agent_sessions.lua <<'EOF'
        -- Private per-project references to agent conversations; never store transcripts.
        local M = {}
        local store = require("fox.core.store")
        local commands = {
        	codex = function(id) return { "codex", "resume", id } end,
        	claude = function(id) return { "claude", "--resume", id } end,
        	opencode = function(id) return { "opencode", "--session", id } end,
        	agy = function(id) return { "agy", "--conversation", id } end,
        }
        
        local function valid_session(session)
        	return type(session) == "table"
        		and commands[session.agent] ~= nil
        		and type(session.id) == "string"
        		and #session.id > 0
        		and #session.id <= 256
        		and session.id:match("^[%w_%-]+$") ~= nil
        		and type(session.slot) == "number"
        		and session.slot >= 1
        		and session.slot <= 9
        		and session.slot % 1 == 0
        end
        
        function M.file(root)
        	return vim.fn.stdpath("state") .. "/fox/agent-sessions/" .. vim.fn.sha256(vim.fs.normalize(root)) .. ".json"
        end
        
        function M.load(root)
        	local data = store.load(M.file(root), {})
        	if data.root ~= vim.fs.normalize(root) or type(data.sessions) ~= "table" then
        		return {}
        	end
        	local sessions, used = {}, {}
        	for _, session in ipairs(data.sessions) do
        		if valid_session(session) and not used[session.slot] then
        			used[session.slot] = true
        			sessions[#sessions + 1] = { agent = session.agent, id = session.id, slot = session.slot }
        		end
        	end
        	table.sort(sessions, function(a, b) return a.slot < b.slot end)
        	return sessions
        end
        
        function M.save(root, sessions)
        	local normalized = vim.fs.normalize(root)
        	local used = {}
        	for _, session in ipairs(sessions) do
        		if not valid_session(session) or used[session.slot] then
        			return false, "Invalid or duplicate agent session"
        		end
        		used[session.slot] = true
        	end
        	local directory = vim.fn.fnamemodify(M.file(root), ":h")
        	vim.fn.mkdir(directory, "p", 448)
        	local temporary = M.file(root) .. "." .. vim.fn.getpid() .. ".tmp"
        	local ok, err = store.save(temporary, { root = normalized, sessions = sessions })
        	if not ok then return false, err end
        	vim.uv.fs_chmod(temporary, 384)
        	local renamed, rename_err = vim.uv.fs_rename(temporary, M.file(root))
        	if not renamed then vim.uv.fs_unlink(temporary); return false, rename_err end
        	return true
        end
        
        function M.resume_command(session)
        	return valid_session(session) and commands[session.agent](session.id) or nil
        end
        
        function M.compact(sessions)
        	local compacted = vim.deepcopy(sessions)
        	table.sort(compacted, function(a, b) return a.slot < b.slot end)
        	for slot, session in ipairs(compacted) do session.slot = slot end
        	return compacted
        end
        
        return M
        EOF

    ---
    name: ponytail
    description: >
      Lazy senior dev mode: the smallest change that fully solves the task, and a
      reply a busy human understands in one read. Use on any coding task (writing,
      fixing, refactoring, reviewing, choosing dependencies) and when the user says
      "ponytail", "be lazy", "simplest solution", "yagni", or complains about
      over-engineering or bloat. Levels: lite, full (default), ultra.
    argument-hint: "[lite|full|ultra|off]"
    license: MIT
    ---
    
    # Ponytail
    
    You are a lazy senior developer. The best code is the code never written. You solve the whole problem with the least new code. End your reply with one or two lines: what you skipped or did not check, and any risk the user must know.
    
    Active for the whole session until the user says "stop ponytail" or "normal mode". Switch level: `/ponytail lite|full|ultra`.
    
    ## Before you write
    
    Read the task and the code it touches. List every place your change must reach: callers, tests, fixtures, config, exports. Check what your change could break for users: data it would destroy or expose, callers that stop working. That is scope. Extra features are not.
    
    ## The smallest complete change
    
    Take the first option that fully works:
    
    1. Does it need to exist? Skip features, options and flexibility nobody asked for, and name them in one line. A vague request ("build me X") gets the smallest version that does the core job.
    2. Already in this codebase (a helper, component, service, pattern)? Use it the way the surrounding code does.
    3. Standard library or a platform feature? Use it, unless the project has its own. A house component beats a native widget.
    4. An installed dependency? Use it. Never add a dependency for a few lines.
    5. Can it be one line a reader gets at a glance? One line.
    6. Otherwise: the minimum code that works.
    
    - Be lazy about the solution, never about the change itself: finish every part the task needs, including the callers, tests and fixtures your change breaks.
    - No abstraction, wrapper, type conversion, option, config, boilerplate or "for later" code nobody asked for. Keep values in the form the platform already gives you. Deletion beats addition. Keep the structure the codebase already has: its layers, interfaces and conventions.
    - Minimize the complete diff across all affected source files, preserving existing code, style, names and structure wherever they already work. Do not rewrite, reformat, modernize or switch patterns for a supposedly better approach unless explicitly requested or necessary to meet the task, correctness or safety. Do not count generated files when assessing diff size. A one-liner that needs decoding is not short.
    - Comment only the why the code cannot show, in one line.
    - Bug fix: before you edit, grep every caller of the function you touch, then fix the root cause once in the shared code.
    - Code you move or merge keeps its error handling and validation.
    - Between options of equal size, take the one that is correct on edge cases.
    - Lazy code without its check is unfinished: new non-trivial logic (a branch, a loop, a parser, money or security, or a whole new script or app) leaves one small test or an assert-based self-check. Trivial changes need none.
    - A shortcut with a known limit gets a code comment in this form: `shortcut: <the limit>, <when to upgrade>`.
    
    Never cut: validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs, anything the user asked for.
    
    ## Levels
    
    | Level | Behavior |
    |-------|----------|
    | **lite** | Build what was asked. Name the smaller option in one line and let the user pick. |
    | **full** | The rules above. Default. |
    | **ultra** | Also question the request: before building, push back on any part the need does not justify. |
    
    
    Default: "full"
    
    ---
    name: kl-solid-coding
    description: "Apply SOLID and KL's selected Go proverbs on every coding task in any language: write, fix, refactor, review, choose dependencies. Always use alongside Ponytail."
    metadata:
      display-name: KL-SOLID-CODING
    ---
    
    # SOLID coding
    
    # ALWAYS
    
    - Every coding task: load [Ponytail](../ponytail/SKILL.md) + relevant language/domain skills.
    - Current need; Ponytail's smallest complete solution. Go concurrency/panic guidance requires equivalent language semantics.
    
    ## SOLID meanings
    
    - **S — Single Responsibility Principle:** one reason to change per module, tied to one actor/business concern; group code changing together, separate independent concerns.
    - **O — Open/Closed Principle:** open for extension, closed for modification; extend stable boundaries without changing established behavior. Extension points require demonstrated variation.
    - **L — Liskov Substitution Principle:** substitutes preserve contracts: accepted inputs, outputs, invariants, failure behavior; no stronger preconditions/weaker postconditions.
    - **I — Interface Segregation Principle:** clients depend only on needed operations; small, cohesive, consumer-focused contracts.
    - **D — Dependency Inversion Principle:** high-level policy + low-level details depend on abstractions; details implement policy-facing contracts. Supply concrete dependencies at composition boundaries.
    
    ## Go proverbs
    
    - Don't communicate by sharing memory, share memory by communicating.
    - Concurrency is not parallelism.
    - Channels orchestrate; mutexes serialize.
    - The bigger the interface, the weaker the abstraction.
    - A little copying is better than a little dependency.
    - Clear is better than clever.
    - Reflection is never clear.
    - Don't just check errors, handle them gracefully.
    - Design the architecture, name the components, document the details.
    - Don't panic.
    
    # NEVER
    
    - No speculative interfaces, inheritance, layers, or dependencies for SOLID compliance; preserve required contracts, validation, error handling.
    
    # Taste
    
    - Use names that state the domain purpose of methods, variables and collections; avoid unexplained names such as `rules` or `reglas` outside framework-required contracts. Preserve framework names and existing public contracts.
    - Prefer consistent domain-first prefixes that group related variables/settings like namespaces, following the language casing convention. Example: `panel_zoom_enabled`, `panel_zoom_gesture_type`, `panel_zoom_factor`, `panel_zoom_slider_min`; keep the distinct panel-opening setting `panel_gesture` separate. Avoid reversed or ambiguous names such as `panel_gesture_zoom`. When renaming persisted keys, migrate existing values and update all callers.
    - Keep helpers private/file-local and at the end of their class or file. Shared behavior belongs in a named domain collaborator, not cross-file `helper_*` calls or generic utility bags.
    - Avoid unexplained numeric IDs and abstract values in conditions. Prefer meaningful strings where the domain permits; otherwise use a named constant or a preceding comment explaining the case. Preserve required external IDs.
    - PHP: import classes at the top of the file instead of repeating fully qualified class names inside methods, unless qualification is needed to disambiguate.
    - Add PHPDoc for non-obvious contracts, data shapes, constraints and business reasons; do not restate the signature or narrate the implementation. Check history when assessing stale comments; retain explanations of current constraints.
    - Keep related validation and special cases understandable together: do not silently skip several fields and handle them elsewhere under an unexplained check for only one of them.
    - Prefer a little local duplication over a dependency on a trivial wrapper that adds no meaningful responsibility. Do not extract a module solely to wrap a few lines.
    - Keep behavior specific to one case in its owning module or screen. Extract it only for demonstrated reuse or a boundary that materially improves clarity or correctness; one usage alone does not justify another file or layer.
    - Place reusable components in the project's component area, grouped in subfolders by module, domain or category. Keep screens in the screen area and compose them from those components. Apply these rules independently of language or framework.
    ---
    name: kl-backend
    description: "Apply KL's backend architecture and testing preferences when building, reviewing, or refactoring services, APIs, workers, and bots in any language or framework: explicit constructor injection, thin entry points, real service tests, and replaceable IO."
    metadata:
      display-name: KL-backend
      version: "1.0.0"
    ---
    
    # Backend
    
    Backend architecture, DI, persistence, tests; project-native mechanisms.
    
    Conditions/exceptions apply; preferences remain preferences.
    
    # ALWAYS
    
    - Explicit constructor/initializer dependencies; inject DB, filesystem, network, collaborators. Native DI when available; resolve at composition roots/test setup. Centralize identifiers; separate production/test wiring; class types where supported, tokens for interfaces/config/ambiguity.
    - Container builds service graph, including tests; override changed IO/config. Small contracts for replaceable IO; concrete services when interfaces add no substitution value.
    - Deliberate lifetimes: infrastructure/intentional queues may be singletons; isolate per-execution context. Typed data-service facade optional when simplifying callers.
    - Thin controllers/commands/handlers/jobs; cohesive services; testable stateless calculation/formatting helpers. Existing lifecycle base class, otherwise native middleware/guards/interceptors; inheritance only for justified shared lifecycle.
    - Map differing DB/domain names, nullability, booleans, encodings at persistence boundary. Inject connection into data services; direct ORM services acceptable.
    - Preserve expected absence/rejection versus unexpected failure and existing result contracts. Validate untrusted entry input; useful error boundary, diagnostics, caller feedback. Promised atomic multi-write transition → datastore transaction.
    - Interactive entrypoints: meaningful progress/success/failure feedback. Localization optional unless requested/existing.
    - Colocated tests, project runner; observable outcomes + important failure paths. Persistence: prefer real services/queries, isolated DB, actual schema/migrations; ask SQLite versus project DB when choosing. In-memory DB only with compatible semantics; actual engine when constraints/transactions/dialect matter.
    - Explicit fixtures via reusable domain builders/seed helpers; reset mutable data per test, retain/reseed lookups. Shared resettable DB → sequential tests or separate parallel DBs.
    - Reuse test composition; override DB/external IO. Resolve subject through child container/native testing module; bind resolved instance only when assertions require identical instance. Testing modules: import relevant module, override external providers before compilation, then resolve.
    - Fake filesystem/messaging/remote APIs at boundaries; real subject/business collaborators. Pure dependency-free helpers may be instantiated directly.
    - Drive input boundary; assert responses/events/messages + persisted state. Relevant invalid input, missing data, rejection/cancellation, permissions covered.
    - Public lifecycle cleanup: queues, timers, spies/clocks, resources; singleton state needs separate reset. Native provider registration/tokens/overrides/lifecycle; native request/auth extension points.
    
    # NEVER
    
    - No business-logic global dependency lookup, injected service-locator container, second DI container, or parallel manually reconstructed test graph.
    - No request data in singleton fields; child container ≠ state isolation. Grouped facade ≠ transaction or mandatory layer.
    - No unnecessary IO interfaces, generic repositories, duplicate CRUD layers, or unjustified inheritance; no mandated language/ORM/DI/runner/framework.
    - No leaked persistence conversions/sensitive public error internals; infrastructure failure ≠ success.
    - No private-method/exact-call-sequence tests or private-collection cleanup. No handwritten RAM repositories replacing realistic persistence tests. DB row reset ≠ singleton reset.
    
    # Taste
    
    - Tabular/report data: preserve named fields through queries, preparation, templates and exports. Define columns by field name, with display/export order explicit; avoid discarding keys and matching values to headers/styles by numeric position. Preserve external positional formats at their serialization boundary.

    -- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    -- Writing
    -- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    
    --- Writes a string to a file, creating parent directories as needed.
    ---
    --- @param filepath string Destination path.
    --- @param content string Content to write.
    --- @return boolean ok
    --- @return string|nil err Error message when `ok` is false.
    function M.write_file(filepath, content)
        path_util.ensure_dir(vim.fs.dirname(filepath))
        local f, err = io.open(filepath, "w")
        if not f then
            return false, err or ("cannot open for writing: " .. filepath)
        end
        f:write(content)
        f:close()
        return true
    end
    
    --- Encodes a table as JSON and writes it, creating parent directories.
    ---
    --- @param filepath string Destination path.
    --- @param data table Table to serialize.
    --- @return boolean ok
    --- @return string|nil err Error message when `ok` is false.
    function M.save(filepath, data)
        if type(data) ~= "table" then
            return false, "store.save expects a table, got " .. type(data)
        end
        local ok, encoded = pcall(encode_sorted, data)
        if not ok then
            return false, tostring(encoded)
        end
        return M.write_file(filepath, encoded)
    end
    
    return M

    879:function M.setup()
    -- ============================================================================
    -- FOX PLUGIN: Multi-Terminal -- nine lazily created terminals in one dock slot.
    -- ============================================================================
    -- WHAT IT DOES
    --   1. Manages `M.settings.count` independent terminals, created on first use.
    --   2. <A-1>..<A-9> selects a terminal and shows it in the dock's terminal pane.
    --   3. <C-;> / <A-;> toggles the SELECTED terminal open and hidden.
    --   4. Remembers the code window you came from, so closing returns focus there.
    --   5. Survives a config reload: the terminal table lives on `_G`, and
    --      `sync_terminals` re-adopts terminal buffers that lost their slot.
    --
    -- WHY BUFFER VARIABLES
    --   `fox_term_num` (slot) and `fox_is_multi_term` (ownership) live on the buffer
    --   itself, so a reload, a session restore or a stray `:terminal` can be mapped
    --   back to a slot without keeping a parallel registry in sync.
    --
    -- COLLABORATORS
    --   fox.core.dock      Bottom dock shared with the task runner.
    --   plugins.fox.tools.wsl    Chooses the WSL shell when the project lives under WSL.
    -- ============================================================================
    
    local lazy_req = require("fox.core.lazy_require")
    local dock = lazy_req("fox.core.dock")
    local store = lazy_req("fox.core.store")
    
    local M = {}
    
    -- ============================================================================
    -- CONFIGURATION
    -- ============================================================================
    
    M.settings = {
        --- How many terminals exist. `<A-n>` is bound for each.
        count = 9,
    
        --- Height of a freshly opened terminal split, when nothing is saved yet.
        default_height = 10,
    
        --- Accepted range for the remembered height; anything else is ignored.
        min_height = 3,
        max_height = 100,
    
        --- Where the last used terminal height is remembered.
        height_file = vim.fn.stdpath("state") .. "/terminal_height",
    
        --- Where the last used centered floating terminal height is remembered.

## Assistant

Estoy conectando cada terminal con un identificador propio y un registro privado por proyecto, para que dos agentes del mismo tipo no se confundan. El agente paralelo prepara la captura compatible con las configuraciones existentes de cada CLI.

        cat > lua/fox/terminal/agent_sessions.lua <<'EOF'
        local M = {}
        local store = require("fox.core.store")
        local uv = vim.uv or vim.loop
        local commands = {
        	codex = { "codex", "resume" },
        	claude = { "claude", "--resume" },
        	opencode = { "opencode", "--session" },
        	agy = { "agy", "--conversation" },
        }
        
        local function valid(session)
        	return type(session) == "table"
        		and commands[session.agent] ~= nil
        		and type(session.id) == "string"
        		and #session.id > 0
        		and #session.id <= 256
        		and session.id:match("^[%w_%-]+$") ~= nil
        		and type(session.slot) == "number"
        		and session.slot >= 1
        		and session.slot <= 9
        		and session.slot % 1 == 0
        end
        
        function M.file(root)
        	return vim.fn.stdpath("state") .. "/fox/agent-sessions/" .. vim.fn.sha256(vim.fs.normalize(root)) .. ".json"
        end
        
        function M.load(root)
        	local data = store.load(M.file(root), {})
        	if data.root ~= vim.fs.normalize(root) or type(data.sessions) ~= "table" then
        		return {}
        	end
        	local sessions, used = {}, {}
        	for _, session in ipairs(data.sessions) do
        		if valid(session) and not used[session.slot] then
        			used[session.slot] = true
        			sessions[#sessions + 1] = { agent = session.agent, id = session.id, slot = session.slot }
        		end
        	end
        	table.sort(sessions, function(a, b) return a.slot < b.slot end)
        	return sessions
        end
        
        function M.save(root, sessions)
        	local used = {}
        	for _, session in ipairs(sessions) do
        		if not valid(session) or used[session.slot] then
        			return false, "Invalid or duplicate agent session"
        		end
        		used[session.slot] = true
        	end
        	local filename = M.file(root)
        	vim.fn.mkdir(vim.fs.dirname(filename), "p", 448)
        	local temporary = filename .. "." .. vim.fn.getpid() .. ".tmp"
        	local ok, err = store.save(temporary, { root = vim.fs.normalize(root), sessions = sessions })
        	if not ok then return false, err end
        	uv.fs_chmod(temporary, 384)
        	local renamed, rename_err = uv.fs_rename(temporary, filename)
        	if not renamed then uv.fs_unlink(temporary); return false, rename_err end
        	return true
        end
        
        function M.command(session)
        	if not valid(session) then return nil end
        	local command = vim.list_extend({}, commands[session.agent])
        	command[#command + 1] = session.id
        	return command
        end
        
        function M.compact(sessions)
        	local result = vim.deepcopy(sessions)
        	table.sort(result, function(a, b) return a.slot < b.slot end)
        	for slot, session in ipairs(result) do session.slot = slot end
        	return result
        end
        
        return M
        EOF

        local t = require("fox.lib.foxnvim.test")
        local sessions = require("fox.terminal.agent_sessions")
        
        t.describe("agent session persistence", function()
        	t.it("keeps exact IDs isolated by project and compacts slots", function()
        		local original, dir = sessions.file, vim.fn.tempname()
        		vim.fn.mkdir(dir, "p")
        		sessions.file = function(root) return dir .. "/" .. vim.fn.sha256(root) .. ".json" end
        		local data = { { slot = 5, agent = "claude", id = "claude-id" }, { slot = 1, agent = "codex", id = "codex-id" } }
        		local ok, err = pcall(function()
        			assert(sessions.save("/project/a", data))
        			t.expect(sessions.load("/project/b")).toEqual({})
        			local compacted = sessions.compact(sessions.load("/project/a"))
        			t.expect(compacted).toEqual({ { slot = 1, agent = "codex", id = "codex-id" }, { slot = 2, agent = "claude", id = "claude-id" } })
        			t.expect(data[1].slot).toBe(5)
        			t.expect(sessions.command(compacted[1])).toEqual({ "codex", "resume", "codex-id" })
        			t.expect(sessions.command({ slot = 1, agent = "agy", id = "x;rm" })).toBeNil()
        			t.expect(sessions.save("/project/a", { data[1], data[1] })).toBe(false)
        			t.expect(#sessions.load("/project/a")).toBe(2)
        		end)
        		sessions.file = original
        		vim.fn.delete(dir, "rf")
        		assert(ok, err)
        	end)
        end)

    -- Invoked by agent hooks with Neovim -l; no user configuration is loaded.
    local agent = arg[1]
    local directory, terminal = vim.env.FOXVIM_AGENT_EVENT_DIR, vim.env.FOXVIM_TERMINAL_ID
    local function report()
        if not directory or not terminal or not terminal:match("^[%w_-]+$") then
            return
        end
        local payload = vim.json.decode(io.read("*a"))
        if type(payload) ~= "table" then
            return
        end
        local id = agent == "agy" and payload.conversationId or payload.session_id
        if type(id) ~= "string" or id == "" or #id > 1024 then
            return
        end
        local event = payload.hook_event_name
        if agent ~= "agy" and event ~= "SessionStart" and event ~= "SessionEnd" then
            return
        end
        if payload.agent_id or payload.cursor_version or vim.env.CURSOR_VERSION then
            return
        end
        if agent == "codex" and vim.env.CODEX_THREAD_ID and vim.env.CODEX_THREAD_ID ~= id then
            return
        end
        if agent ~= "codex" and agent ~= "claude" and agent ~= "agy" then
            return
        end
        local target = directory .. "/" .. terminal .. ".json"
        local temporary = target .. "." .. vim.fn.getpid() .. ".tmp"
        local handle = io.open(temporary, "w")
        if not handle then
            return
        end
        local written =
            handle:write(vim.json.encode({ agent = agent, id = id, event = event == "SessionEnd" and "end" or "start" }))
        local closed = handle:close()
        if not written or not closed or not os.rename(temporary, target) then
            os.remove(temporary)
        end
    end
    pcall(report)
    if agent == "agy" then
        io.write("{}\n")
    end
    local M = {}
    local asset_directory = vim.fn.fnamemodify(debug.getinfo(1, "S").source:sub(2), ":h")
    
    local function read(path)
        local handle = io.open(path, "rb")
        if not handle then
            if vim.uv.fs_stat(path) then
                error("Cannot read " .. path)
            end
            return nil
        end
        local content = handle:read("*a")
        handle:close()
        return content
    end
    
    local function write(path, content)
        local old = read(path)
        if old == content then
            return
        end
        vim.fn.mkdir(vim.fs.dirname(path), "p")
        local function save(target, text)
            local handle = assert(io.open(target, "wb"))
            assert(handle:write(text))
            assert(handle:close())
        end
        if old and not vim.uv.fs_stat(path .. ".foxvim-backup") then
            save(path .. ".foxvim-backup", old)
        end
        local temporary = path .. ".foxvim-" .. vim.fn.getpid() .. ".tmp"
        save(temporary, content)
        local ok, err = os.rename(temporary, path)
        if not ok then
            os.remove(temporary)
            error(err)
        end
    end
    
    local function object(path)
        local text = read(path)
        local result = text and vim.json.decode(text) or vim.empty_dict()
        assert(type(result) == "table" and (not vim.islist(result) or next(result) == nil), "Expected JSON object: " .. path)
        return result
    end
    
    local function hook_command(agent, options)
        local windows = options.windows
        if windows == nil then
            windows = vim.fn.has("win32") == 1
        end
        local function quote(value)
            if windows then
                assert(not value:find('["%%\r\n]'), "Unsupported Windows hook path")
                return '"' .. value .. '"'
            end
            return "'" .. value:gsub("'", "'\\''") .. "'"
        end
        return quote(options.nvim or vim.v.progpath)
            .. " --headless -u NONE -l "
            .. quote(asset_directory .. "/session_hook.lua")
            .. " "
            .. agent
    end
    
    local function add_session_hook(config, event, command)
        config.hooks = config.hooks or vim.empty_dict()
        assert(
            type(config.hooks) == "table" and (not vim.islist(config.hooks) or next(config.hooks) == nil),
            "hooks must be an object"
        )
        local entries = config.hooks[event] or {}
        assert(type(entries) == "table" and vim.islist(entries), event .. " must be an array")
        for _, entry in ipairs(entries) do
            for _, hook in ipairs(type(entry) == "table" and type(entry.hooks) == "table" and entry.hooks or {}) do
                if hook.command == command then
                    return
                end
            end
        end
        entries[#entries + 1] = { hooks = { { type = "command", command = command, timeout = 10 } } }
        config.hooks[event] = entries
    end
    
    local function codex_features(content)
        local lines = vim.split(content or "", "\n", { plain = true })
        local section, features_index, hooks_index
        for index, line in ipairs(lines) do
            local header = line:match("^%s*%[([^%]]+)%]%s*#?.*$")
            if header then
                section = header
                if section == "features" then
                    features_index = index
                end
            elseif section == "features" and line:match("^%s*hooks%s*=") then
                hooks_index = index
            end
        end
        if hooks_index then
            lines[hooks_index] = "hooks = true"
        elseif features_index then
            table.insert(lines, features_index + 1, "hooks = true")
        else
            lines[#lines + 1] = "[features]"
            lines[#lines + 1] = "hooks = true"
        end
        return table.concat(lines, "\n"):gsub("\n*$", "\n")
    end
    
    --- Explicit installation only. options.home/dirs allow isolated installation/tests.
    --- Existing JSON values survive; malformed JSON/JSONC is refused before writing.
    function M.install(agent, options)
        options = options or {}
        local home = options.home or vim.fn.expand("~")
        local directories = {
            codex = (not options.home and vim.env.CODEX_HOME) or home .. "/.codex",
            claude = (not options.home and vim.env.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR) or home .. "/.claude",
            opencode = (not options.home and vim.env.XDG_CONFIG_HOME or home .. "/.config") .. "/opencode",
            agy = home .. "/.gemini/config",
        }
        local directory = options.dirs and options.dirs[agent] or directories[agent]
        assert(directory, "Unknown agent: " .. tostring(agent))
        local changed = {}
        local function save(path, content)
            write(path, content)
            changed[#changed + 1] = path
        end
        if agent == "opencode" then
            assert(
                not read(directory .. "/tui.jsonc"),
                "OpenCode tui.jsonc requires manual plugin registration; existing JSONC was preserved"
            )
            local tui_path, cli_path = directory .. "/tui.json", directory .. "/cli.json"
            local tui, cli = object(tui_path), object(cli_path)
            for _, item in ipairs({ { tui, "./foxvim-session.js" }, { cli, "./foxvim-session" } }) do
                item[1].plugin = item[1].plugin or {}
                assert(vim.islist(item[1].plugin), "OpenCode plugin must be an array")
                if not vim.tbl_contains(item[1].plugin, item[2]) then
                    table.insert(item[1].plugin, item[2])
                end
            end
            local plugin_path = directory .. "/foxvim-session.js"
            local existing = read(plugin_path)
            assert(
                not existing or existing:find("FoxVim session integration", 1, true),
                "Refusing to overwrite " .. plugin_path
            )
            local entry_path = directory .. "/foxvim-session/tui.js"
            local entry = read(entry_path)
            assert(not entry or entry:find("FoxVim session integration", 1, true), "Refusing to overwrite " .. entry_path)
            save(plugin_path, assert(read(asset_directory .. "/opencode_hook.js")))
            save(entry_path, '// FoxVim session integration\nexport { default } from "../foxvim-session.js";\n')
            save(tui_path, vim.json.encode(tui) .. "\n")
            save(cli_path, vim.json.encode(cli) .. "\n")
        else
            local path = directory .. (agent == "claude" and "/settings.json" or "/hooks.json")
            local config = object(path)
            local command = hook_command(agent, options)
            if agent == "agy" then
                assert(config.foxvim == nil or type(config.foxvim) == "table", "foxvim hook block must be an object")
                config.foxvim = config.foxvim or vim.empty_dict()
                config.foxvim.PreInvocation = { { type = "command", command = command, timeout = 10 } }
            else
                add_session_hook(config, "SessionStart", command)
                if agent == "claude" then
                    add_session_hook(config, "SessionEnd", command)
                end
            end
            local toml_path = directory .. "/config.toml"
            local toml = agent == "codex" and codex_features(read(toml_path)) or nil
            save(path, vim.json.encode(config) .. "\n")
            if toml then
                save(toml_path, toml)
            end
        end
        return changed
    end
    
    return M
    // FoxVim session integration; managed asset, no external packages.
    import { writeFileSync, renameSync, unlinkSync } from "node:fs";
    import { join } from "node:path";
    
    function report(id) {
      const directory = process.env.FOXVIM_AGENT_EVENT_DIR;
      const terminal = process.env.FOXVIM_TERMINAL_ID;
      if (!directory || !terminal || !/^[\w-]+$/.test(terminal) || typeof id !== "string" || !id || id.length > 1024) return;
      const target = join(directory, `${terminal}.json`);
      const temporary = `${target}.${process.pid}.tmp`;
      try {
        writeFileSync(temporary, JSON.stringify({ agent: "opencode", id, event: "start" }), { mode: 0o600 });
        renameSync(temporary, target);
      } catch {
        try { unlinkSync(temporary); } catch {}
      }
    }
    
    function watch(selection) {
      let previous;
      const update = () => {
        const id = selection();
        if (id && id !== previous) { report(id); previous = id; }
      };
      update();
      const timer = setInterval(update, 100);
      timer.unref?.();
      return () => clearInterval(timer);
    }
    
    // Both OpenCode TUI SDKs provide the selected session; server-global events
    // cannot safely attribute an attached TUI to a FoxVim terminal.
    export default {
      id: "foxvim.session",
      setup(api) {
        if (!process.env.FOXVIM_AGENT_EVENT_DIR) return;
        return watch(() => {
          const route = api.ui.router.current();
          return route.type === "session" ? route.sessionID : undefined;
        });
      },
      tui(api) {
        if (!process.env.FOXVIM_AGENT_EVENT_DIR) return;
        const dispose = watch(() => {
          const route = api.route.current;
          return route?.name === "session" ? route.params?.sessionID : undefined;
        });
        api.lifecycle.onDispose(dispose);
      },
    };

    -- ============================================================================
    -- STATE -- kept on _G so a config reload does not orphan open terminals
    -- ============================================================================
    
    _G._fox_terminals = _G._fox_terminals or {}
    _G._fox_selected_terminal = _G._fox_selected_terminal or 1
    
    --- `[n] = { buf = integer|nil, win = integer|nil }`
    local terminals = _G._fox_terminals
    
    --- Window to return to when the terminal is dismissed.
    local code_win = nil
    
    --- Current terminal split height, persisted across sessions.
    local terminal_height_cached = nil
    local agent_sessions = require("fox.terminal.agent_sessions")
    local agent_terminals, agent_restored = {}, {}
    local agent_event_dir = vim.fn.stdpath("state")
        .. "/fox/agent-events/"
        .. vim.fn.getpid()
        .. "-"
        .. tostring((vim.uv or vim.loop).hrtime())
    local agent_shutdown = false
    
    local function agent_token()
        return tostring(vim.fn.getpid()) .. "-" .. tostring((vim.uv or vim.loop).hrtime())
    end
    
    local function agent_environment(token)
        return {
            FOXVIM_AGENT_EVENT_DIR = agent_event_dir,
            FOXVIM_TERMINAL_ID = token,
            FOXVIM_NVIM_EXECUTABLE = vim.v.progpath,
        }
    end
    
    local function save_agent_terminals(root)
        local saved = agent_sessions.load(root)
        for _, tracked in pairs(agent_terminals) do
            if tracked.root == root and tracked.saved_slot then
                for i = #saved, 1, -1 do
                    if saved[i].slot == tracked.saved_slot then
                        table.remove(saved, i)
                    end
                end
            end
        end
        for _, tracked in pairs(agent_terminals) do
            if tracked.root == root and tracked.session and vim.api.nvim_buf_is_valid(tracked.buf) then
                local slot = vim.b[tracked.buf].fox_term_num
                for i = #saved, 1, -1 do
                    if saved[i].slot == slot then
                        table.remove(saved, i)
                    end
                end
                saved[#saved + 1] = { slot = slot, agent = tracked.session.agent, id = tracked.session.id }
                tracked.saved_slot = slot
            else
                tracked.saved_slot = tracked.root == root and nil or tracked.saved_slot
            end
        end
        local ok, err = agent_sessions.save(root, saved)
        if not ok then
            vim.notify("Could not save agent sessions: " .. tostring(err), vim.log.levels.ERROR)
        end
    end
    
    local function poll_agent_events()
        local changed = {}
        for token, tracked in pairs(agent_terminals) do
            local file = agent_event_dir .. "/" .. token .. ".json"
            if (vim.uv or vim.loop).fs_stat(file) then
                local event = store.load(file, {})(vim.uv or vim.loop).fs_unlink(file)
                if event.event == "end" and not agent_shutdown then
                    tracked.session = nil
                    changed[tracked.root] = true
                elseif agent_sessions.command({ slot = 1, agent = event.agent, id = event.id }) then
                    tracked.session = { agent = event.agent, id = event.id }
                    changed[tracked.root] = true
                end
            end
        end
        for root in pairs(changed) do
            save_agent_terminals(root)
        end
    end
    
    -- ============================================================================
    -- HEIGHT PERSISTENCE
    -- ============================================================================
    
    --- Reads the remembered height, falling back to the default.
    --- @return integer height
    local function load_saved_height()
        local raw = store.read_file(M.settings.height_file)
        local height = raw and tonumber(raw) or nil
        if height and height >= M.settings.min_height and height <= M.settings.max_height then
            return height
        end
        return M.settings.default_height
    end
    
    local function get_terminal_height()
        if not terminal_height_cached then
            terminal_height_cached = load_saved_height()
        end
        return terminal_height_cached
    end
    
    --- Remembers a new height when it is in range and actually changed.
        local new_h = math.min(max_h, math.max(M.settings.min_height, cur_h + delta))
    
        if new_h ~= cur_h then
            pcall(vim.api.nvim_win_set_height, target_win, new_h)
            save_height(new_h)
            return true
        end
    
        return false
    end
    
    --- Stretches the terminal height larger (<C-Up>).
    function M.increase_height()
        local step = M.settings.resize_step or 2
        M.resize_height(step)
    end
    
    --- Shrinks the terminal height smaller (<C-Down>).
    function M.decrease_height()
        local step = M.settings.resize_step or 2
        M.resize_height(-step)
    end
    
    --- Re-exported for callers that relied on this module owning the dock order.
    M.enforce_bottom_layout = dock.enforce_order
    
    -- ============================================================================
    -- SETUP
    -- ============================================================================
    
    --- Pastes the OS clipboard into a terminal (`"+`, falling back to `"*`).
    local function paste_clipboard()
        local clip = vim.fn.getreg("+")
        if not clip or clip == "" then
            clip = vim.fn.getreg("*")
        end
        if clip and clip ~= "" then
            vim.api.nvim_paste(clip, true, -1)
        end
    end
    
    local function restore_agent_terminals(root)
        root = vim.fs.normalize(root)
        if agent_restored[root] then
            return
        end
        agent_restored[root] = true
        local saved = agent_sessions.compact(agent_sessions.load(root))
        if #saved == 0 then
            return
        end
        sync_terminals()
        for _, session in ipairs(saved) do
            local term = get_term(session.slot)
            if not is_valid_buf(term.buf) then
                local command = agent_sessions.command(session)
                if vim.fn.executable(command[1]) == 1 then
                    local buf, token = vim.api.nvim_create_buf(false, true), agent_token()
                    vim.b[buf].fox_term_num, vim.b[buf].fox_is_multi_term = session.slot, true
                    vim.b[buf].fox_env_slot = _G._fox_active_env_slot or 1
                    local job
                    vim.api.nvim_buf_call(buf, function()
                        job = vim.fn.jobstart(command, { term = true, cwd = root, env = agent_environment(token) })
                    end)
                    if job > 0 then
                        term.buf = buf
                        agent_terminals[token] =
                            { buf = buf, root = root, session = { agent = session.agent, id = session.id }, saved_slot = session.slot }
                    else
                        vim.api.nvim_buf_delete(buf, { force = true })
                        vim.notify("Could not resume " .. session.agent, vim.log.levels.ERROR)
                    end
                else
                    vim.notify("Cannot resume " .. session.agent .. ": executable not found", vim.log.levels.WARN)
                end
            end
        end
        local ok, err = agent_sessions.save(root, saved)
        if not ok then
            vim.notify("Could not save agent sessions: " .. tostring(err), vim.log.levels.ERROR)
        end
    end

    tests/spec/agent_sessions_spec.lua
    tests/terminal_hooks.test.mjs
    local M = {}
    local asset_directory = vim.fn.fnamemodify(debug.getinfo(1, "S").source:sub(2), ":h")
    
    local function read(path)
        local handle = io.open(path, "rb")
        if not handle then
            if vim.uv.fs_stat(path) then
                error("Cannot read " .. path)
            end
            return nil
        end
        local content = handle:read("*a")
        handle:close()
        return content
    end
    
    local function write(path, content)
        local old = read(path)
        if old == content then
            return
        end
        vim.fn.mkdir(vim.fs.dirname(path), "p")
        local function save(target, text)
            local handle = assert(io.open(target, "wb"))
            assert(handle:write(text))
            assert(handle:close())
        end
        if old and not vim.uv.fs_stat(path .. ".foxvim-backup") then
            save(path .. ".foxvim-backup", old)
        end
        local temporary = path .. ".foxvim-" .. vim.fn.getpid() .. ".tmp"
        save(temporary, content)
        local ok, err = vim.uv.fs_rename(temporary, path)
        if not ok then
            os.remove(temporary)
            error(err)
        end
    end
    
    local function object(path)
        local text = read(path)
        assert(not text or text:match("^%s*{"), "Expected JSON object: " .. path)
        local result = text and vim.json.decode(text) or vim.empty_dict()
        assert(type(result) == "table" and (not vim.islist(result) or next(result) == nil), "Expected JSON object: " .. path)
        return result
    end
    
    local function hook_command(agent, options)
        local function quote(value)
            return "'" .. value:gsub("'", "'\\''") .. "'"
        end
        local windows = options.windows
        if windows == nil then
            windows = vim.fn.has("win32") == 1
        end
        if windows then
            local function powershell_quote(value)
                return "'" .. value:gsub("'", "''") .. "'"
            end
            local command = "& "
                .. powershell_quote(options.nvim or vim.v.progpath)
                .. " --headless -u NONE -l "
                .. powershell_quote(asset_directory .. "/session_hook.lua")
                .. " "
                .. agent
            return "powershell.exe -NoProfile -NonInteractive -EncodedCommand "
                .. vim.base64.encode(vim.iconv(command, "utf-8", "utf-16le"))
        end
        return quote(options.nvim or vim.v.progpath)
            .. " --headless -u NONE -l "
            .. quote(asset_directory .. "/session_hook.lua")
            .. " "
            .. agent
    end
    
    local function add_session_hook(config, event, command)
        config.hooks = config.hooks or vim.empty_dict()
        assert(
            type(config.hooks) == "table" and (not vim.islist(config.hooks) or next(config.hooks) == nil),
            "hooks must be an object"
        )
        local entries = config.hooks[event] or {}
        assert(type(entries) == "table" and vim.islist(entries), event .. " must be an array")
        for _, entry in ipairs(entries) do
            for _, hook in ipairs(type(entry) == "table" and type(entry.hooks) == "table" and entry.hooks or {}) do
                if hook.command == command then
                    return
                end
            end
        end
        entries[#entries + 1] = { hooks = { { type = "command", command = command, timeout = 10 } } }
        config.hooks[event] = entries
    end
    
    local function codex_features(content)
        local lines = vim.split(content or "", "\n", { plain = true })
        local section, features_index, hooks_index
        for index, line in ipairs(lines) do
            local header = line:match("^%s*%[([^%]]+)%]%s*#?.*$")
            if header then
                section = header
                if section == "features" then
                    features_index = index
                end
            elseif section == "features" and line:match("^%s*hooks%s*=") then
                hooks_index = index
            end
        end
        if hooks_index then
            assert(
                lines[hooks_index]:match("=%s*true%s*$") or lines[hooks_index]:match("=%s*true%s*#"),
                "Codex hooks are explicitly disabled or unsupported; config.toml was preserved"
            )
            return content
        elseif features_index then
            table.insert(lines, features_index + 1, "hooks = true")
        else
            lines[#lines + 1] = "[features]"
            lines[#lines + 1] = "hooks = true"
        end
        return table.concat(lines, "\n"):gsub("\n*$", "\n")
    end
    
    --- Explicit installation only. options.home/dirs allow isolated installation/tests.
    --- Existing JSON values survive; malformed JSON/JSONC is refused before writing.
    function M.install(agent, options)
        options = options or {}
        local home = options.home or vim.fn.expand("~")
        local directories = {
            codex = (not options.home and vim.env.CODEX_HOME) or home .. "/.codex",
            claude = (not options.home and vim.env.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR) or home .. "/.claude",
            opencode = (not options.home and vim.env.XDG_CONFIG_HOME or home .. "/.config") .. "/opencode",
            agy = home .. "/.gemini/config",
        }
        local directory = options.dirs and options.dirs[agent] or directories[agent]
        assert(directories[agent] and directory, "Unknown agent: " .. tostring(agent))
        local changed = {}
        local function save(path, content)
            write(path, content)
            changed[#changed + 1] = path
        end
        if agent == "opencode" then
            assert(
                not read(directory .. "/tui.jsonc"),
                "OpenCode tui.jsonc requires manual plugin registration; existing JSONC was preserved"
            )
            local tui_path, cli_path = directory .. "/tui.json", directory .. "/cli.json"
            local state_home = options.state_home or (not options.home and vim.env.XDG_STATE_HOME) or home .. "/.local/state"
            assert(
                read(cli_path) or not (read(tui_path) or read(state_home .. "/opencode/kv.json")),
                "OpenCode V2 preferences migration is pending; start OpenCode once before installing hooks"
            )
            local tui, cli = object(tui_path), object(cli_path)
            for _, item in ipairs({ { tui, "./foxvim-session.js", "plugin" }, { cli, "./foxvim-session", "plugins" } }) do
                item[1][item[3]] = item[1][item[3]] or {}
                assert(vim.islist(item[1][item[3]]), "OpenCode plugin must be an array")
                if not vim.tbl_contains(item[1][item[3]], item[2]) then
                    table.insert(item[1][item[3]], item[2])
                end
            end
            local plugin_path = directory .. "/foxvim-session.js"
            local existing = read(plugin_path)
            assert(
                not existing or existing:find("FoxVim session integration", 1, true),
                "Refusing to overwrite " .. plugin_path
            )
            local entry_path = directory .. "/foxvim-session/tui.js"
            local entry = read(entry_path)
            assert(not entry or entry:find("FoxVim session integration", 1, true), "Refusing to overwrite " .. entry_path)
            save(plugin_path, assert(read(asset_directory .. "/opencode_hook.js")))
            save(entry_path, '// FoxVim session integration\nexport { default } from "../foxvim-session.js";\n')
            save(tui_path, vim.json.encode(tui) .. "\n")
            save(cli_path, vim.json.encode(cli) .. "\n")
        else
            local path = directory .. (agent == "claude" and "/settings.json" or "/hooks.json")
            local config = object(path)
            local command = hook_command(agent, options)
            if agent == "agy" then
                assert(config.foxvim == nil or type(config.foxvim) == "table", "foxvim hook block must be an object")
                config.foxvim = config.foxvim or vim.empty_dict()
                local entries = config.foxvim.PreInvocation or {}
                assert(type(entries) == "table" and vim.islist(entries), "PreInvocation must be an array")
                local exists = false
                for _, entry in ipairs(entries) do
                    if type(entry) == "table" and entry.command == command then
                        exists = true
                    end
                end
                if not exists then
                    entries[#entries + 1] = { type = "command", command = command, timeout = 10 }
                end
                config.foxvim.PreInvocation = entries
            else
                add_session_hook(config, "SessionStart", command)
                if agent == "claude" then
                    add_session_hook(config, "SessionEnd", command)
                end
            end
            local toml_path = directory .. "/config.toml"
            local toml = agent == "codex" and codex_features(read(toml_path)) or nil
            save(path, vim.json.encode(config) .. "\n")
            if toml then
                save(toml_path, toml)
            end
        end
        return changed
    end
    
    return M
    -- Invoked by agent hooks with Neovim -l; no user configuration is loaded.
    local agent = arg[1]
    local directory, terminal = vim.env.FOXVIM_AGENT_EVENT_DIR, vim.env.FOXVIM_TERMINAL_ID
    local function report()
        if not directory or not terminal or not terminal:match("^[%w_-]+$") then
            return
        end
        local payload = vim.json.decode(io.read("*a"))
        if type(payload) ~= "table" then
            return
        end
        local id = agent == "agy" and payload.conversationId or payload.session_id
        if type(id) ~= "string" or id == "" or #id > 1024 then
            return
        end
        local event = payload.hook_event_name
        if agent ~= "agy" and event ~= "SessionStart" and event ~= "SessionEnd" then
            return
        end
        if payload.agent_id or payload.cursor_version or vim.env.CURSOR_VERSION then
            return
        end
        if agent == "codex" and vim.env.CODEX_THREAD_ID and vim.env.CODEX_THREAD_ID ~= id then
            return
        end
        if agent ~= "codex" and agent ~= "claude" and agent ~= "agy" then
            return
        end
        local target = directory .. "/" .. terminal .. ".json"
        local temporary = target .. "." .. vim.fn.getpid() .. ".tmp"
        local handle = io.open(temporary, "w")
        if not handle then
            return
        end
        local written =
            handle:write(vim.json.encode({ agent = agent, id = id, event = event == "SessionEnd" and "end" or "start" }))
        local closed = handle:close()
        if not written or not closed or not vim.uv.fs_rename(temporary, target) then
            os.remove(temporary)
        end
    end
    pcall(report)
    if agent == "agy" then
        io.write("{}\n")
    end
